On main, pricing phase 1 (2026-09-30) added five files (`pricing`, `pricing-floor`, `pricing-copy`, `brand-guardrails` and `admin-deal`), so the pricing branch had 77 files under `tests/` and 1,086 tests before main's secretary change (#225, three more files: `secretary`, `secretary-brief` and `secretary-page`) was merged into it, and 80 files after that merge (80 `*.test.ts` files on its merged tree), whose test count was not recorded. The pricing rules are numbered 241-250 because main numbered the secretary rules 189-212 and the security gates 213-220 first, and 221-240 are reserved for agent-team step 3a (#226). The pricing branch, once main's security gates (#227, `security-gates`) were merged into it on 2026-09-30, counted 81 files and 1,188 tests from `npx vitest run`, the count of main alone above (the docs fixes added no test). Step 3a before the merge of #228 and #229, status on 2026-09-29, after main's security gates (#227) were merged into it: `npm test` ran 81 files under `tests/` and 1165 tests, counted from `npx vitest run` (1162 before the review of the merge added the `earlier_live` records, the batch channel pin and the brand-proposal part of rule 240, first numbered 241, folded into rule 240 when #228 took 241-250 and #213 251-260): main's 76 files with the security gates from the Taskuary study (rules 213-220, `tests/security-gates.test.ts`) plus step 3a's `generate-batch`, `governance-history`, `prompt-stamp`, `activity` and `activity-sources`. Main alone, after the security gates, counted 76 files and 1089 tests, and step 3a, after main's Reem change (#225) was merged into it, 80 files and 1130 tests. Before the security gates, main, after its teams change was merged into phase 1 of Reem the secretary (with its review and Taskuary fixes), counted 75 files and 1058 tests (the Reem branch alone had 70 files and 919 tests). Earlier: Vitest is set up (`npm test`, 77 files and 1057 tests once step 3a was merged with main's teams change: main's 72 files with teams plus step 3a's five. Before that merge step 3a counted 72 files and 909 tests (step 3's single generation pipeline, governance history and prompt stamps, and per-agent activity log on top of main's 67 files and 850 tests after the media change, steps 1 and 2 of the agent-team upgrade, the OAuth rate limits, MCP `save_draft` and the updates and wishlist page; 64 files and 823 tests before `save_draft` and the updates page), and main with teams 72 files (the teams branch's 69 with the media change plus `mcp-save-draft`, `wishlist` and `updates-page`; the teams branch alone counted 55 files and 605 tests, a few seconds), whose test count was not recorded; counted from `npx vitest run`, about eleven seconds; memory store only, no network or model calls; the media tests refuse every `fetch` through `noNetwork()` in `tests/helpers/media.ts`) and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs type-check, lint, unit tests and the Playwright suite on every push and pull request. First green run: commit `21ccd57` on 2026-09-18. Since 2026-09-18 the Vercel Deployment Check "Nashr CI" (Settings > Build and Deployment > Deployment Checks) holds every production deploy until CI passes, so a red run blocks production and a merge takes a few minutes to go live. Rules without a test below are still unverified.

Legend: **test** = pinned by an automated test in `tests/` · **none** = nothing verifies the rule today · **manual** = checked by hand once (date) · **live** = guarded by a production probe.

Refused with nothing changed: expired (`topup_expired`, also on a record not yet settled), voided (`topup_voided`), already refunded (`topup_refunded`), given for nothing (`topup_not_paid`), nothing left (`nothing_left`), credits left not the ones the founder was shown (`left_changed`, with the credits left and the amount now), a transfer dated before the pack's payment (`bad_date`), an unknown pack (`topup_not_found`) or account (`not_found`). Ten charges racing the refund: every credit of the pack is either spent or refunded, once, and no charge spends a refunded credit. `parseTopupRefund` checks the credits shown (whole, 1 to the largest pack), the transfer day (real, not after today in Riyadh, at most a year back) and a required reference (at most 80 characters, control characters stripped) | test (`topup-refunds.test.ts` "refunding a pack's unused credits: the amount and the ledger (rule 460)") | done |

| 472 | Each country's official private-sector Eid holiday is quiet in `workCalendar(cal, zone, now)` (`src/lib/secretary-calendar.ts`, server only, Umm al-Qura through `hijriDate`, corrected per Hijri year by `hijriFix`), with the days in lieu each law gives, and only on the brand's working days (weekend days stay out of `quiet`): SA four days from the day after 29 Ramadan and from Arafah, every weekend day inside them earning a working day after the Eid (HRSD regulations art. 24 II.1); AE 30 Ramadan (in a 30-day month) to 3 Shawwal and Arafah to 12 Dhu al-Hijjah, nothing in lieu (Cabinet Resolution 27/2024); OM 29 Ramadan to 3 Shawwal and 9-12 Dhu al-Hijjah, one day when 1 Shawwal or 10 Dhu al-Hijjah is a Friday (RD 88/2022); KW 1-3 Shawwal and 9-12, nothing (Law 6/2010 art. 68); BH 1-3 Shawwal and 10-12 without Arafah, a day for each Friday (Cabinet Resolution 1/2013); QA three working days from 1 Shawwal and from 10 Dhu al-Hijjah (Law 14/2004 art. 78). So 2027-05-19 is an Eid day nowhere and quiet only in SA (in lieu of the Saturday Arafah), 2027-03-08 (29 Ramadan) only in OM, 2026-03-19 (30 Ramadan, Thursday) in SA, AE and OM; Fitr 1447 gives SA 19, 22-24 March, AE 19-20, OM 18-19 and 22-23, KW 22, BH 22-23, QA 22-24; Arafah 1447 is quiet in SA, AE, OM and KW, and Friday 29 May 2026 earns Sunday 31 May in SA, BH and QA; with 1 Shawwal 1448 moved to 2027-03-10 in Kuwait, 03-09 is Ramadan and Eid is 10-12 March. The Saudi and UAE Eid al-Fitr starts the day before the effective 1 Shawwal when Ramadan, counted from its Umm al-Qura start, ran 30 days or more, else on 1 Shawwal (ruling R26): in 1447 (a 30-day Umm al-Qura Ramadan, 18 February to 19 March 2026) the Saudi holiday is 19 and 22-24 March as before; with Eid announced a day later (`hijriFix` 1447 `shawwal1` 2026-03-21) it starts on Friday 20 March (22-23 Eid, 24-25 in lieu, 19 March a working day), and no Ramadan runs 31 days: the corrected one is 19 February to 20 March (the 30-day cap moves its start); with Eid a day earlier (19 March, a 29-day Ramadan) it starts on Eid; the UAE follows the same start (20 March, a Friday it works); in 1448 (a 29-day Umm al-Qura Ramadan) Eid a day later leaves the Saudi days off as they were. The country follows `zoneOf` at each read (a brand whose city changes from «دبي» to «الرياض» goes from Sat+Sun to Fri+Sat), and the result is memoised in an LRU of 200 keyed by zone, local day and a hash of the settings without `updatedAt`/`updatedBy`, frozen so no caller can change the next caller's copy; a stored weekend that is not 0-3 contiguous days is ignored for the zone's own (a 7-day one returns instead of looping), and stored Ramadan hours that are not valid windows (`[{from: 5}]`, a window that ends before it starts, off the quarter hour, overlapping) read as the default 10:00-15:00, so no clock throws on a Ramadan day (pinned in rule 478). Since the final review (ruling R30) the calendar reads only `hijriFix`'s `shawwal1` and `dhulhijja1`: Ramadan starts on Umm al-Qura's day whatever a stored `ramadan1` says (1448: 8 February 2027), so the 30-day cap can no longer undo a correction (pinned in rule 474) | test (`secretary-calendar.test.ts` "rule 472: Eid holidays per country (official private-sector spans)", eleven tests; each Hijri date and weekday checked with ICU Umm al-Qura in Riyadh; the Saudi and UAE Eid al-Fitr start of ruling R26 and the 30-day cap on a corrected Ramadan in `secretary-away.test.ts` "ruling R26: …", in the rule 487 block) | done; the Saudi Eid days in lieu are placed after the Eid, one choice of the regulation's "before or after" (the employer's), matching HRSD's 2024 Eid al-Adha as reported in the press, not an HRSD page |
| 473 | The other official holidays and their days in lieu, then the owner's changes: QA National Sport Day (2nd Tuesday of February, 2027-02-09) and 18 December; KW 12 Rabi al-Awwal (2026-08-25) and the rest of art. 68; SA National Day on Saturday 2028-09-23 gives Sunday 09-24, on Friday 2022-09-23 Thursday 09-22; BH a Friday holiday gives the next working day (2022-12-18) and a Saturday one does not (2023-12-18 is not quiet, the private-sector rule; the government's circular gave it); AE gives nothing (National Day on Saturday-Sunday 2028-12-02/03 adds no day); OM's announced 2026 list replaces the decree's non-Eid dates (25-26 Nov quiet, 20 Nov not; update `omanYear` each January), and in 2027 the decree's Saturday-Sunday National Day earns Monday 11-22 once. `holidays.add` "YYYY-MM-DD" is quiet that year only, "MM-DD" and "H-MM-DD" every year (25 Ramadan: 2027-03-04 and 2028-02-21), kind `added`; `holidays.remove` takes a default day out before it earns a day in lieu (removing "09-23" also drops 2028-09-24), a removed holiday day is never where a day in lieu lands (removing Eid day 2027-05-18 keeps Wednesday 05-19 for the Saturday Arafah), and removing a day in lieu drops it rather than moving it (removing 05-19, or 05-19 and 05-20, leaves 16-18 only; removing Thursday 2022-09-22, Friday's National Day's day in lieu, leaves 09-22 and 09-25 both working days). `upcomingQuiet` lists the quiet days of the next 365 days in order with their names (Founding Day 2027-02-22, «اليوم الوطني» 2027-09-23) and the next Ramadan (2027-02-08 to 03-08) | test (`secretary-calendar.test.ts` "rule 473: other official holidays and the days given in lieu", twelve tests) | done |
| 474 | `parseCalendar(body, now, zone, login, stored?)` checks the Working days settings field by field and returns them normalised (weekend in run order, Ramadan windows sorted, duplicates dropped) with `updatedAt` and `updatedBy`; refusals have `SecFail`'s shape (`CalFail`, status 400, `field`): a weekend that is not 0-3 unique contiguous days ([5,1], [0,1,2,3], [5,5], [7]) is `weekend_invalid`; Ramadan hours with 3 windows, a time that is not "HH:mm" on hours 00-23 and minutes 00/15/30/45 ("10:10", "", "9:00", "24:00"), from not before to, overlapping windows or an extra key are `ramadan_hours_invalid` (`[]` and touching windows are accepted); a `hijriFix` year after the zone's next Hijri year is `bad_year`, a date more than a day from Umm al-Qura's month start is `hijri_fix_range`, an unknown month key `bad_request`, and so is a new `ramadan1` (ruling R30: only `shawwal1` and `dhulhijja1`, the two the panel writes; the field is `hijriFix.{year}.ramadan1`), while one equal to the `stored` record's is dropped and `workCalendar` ignores a stored one (Ramadan 1448 starts on Umm al-Qura's 2027-02-08); 31 values in `add` or `remove`, a malformed value or a new removal no default day of the coming window matches is `holidays_invalid`, while a removal already in the `stored` record's `remove` is kept though no default produces it any more (ruling R28: Kuwait's 2027-05-18 turned off, then Eid al-Adha 1448 a day earlier, so 14-17 May; the calendar ignores it and 18 May is a working day; turning it on drops it, and a new 2027-05-19 beside it is still refused; a stale stored one before today-30 is still dropped); an unknown top-level field is `bad_request` (`v`, `updatedAt`, `updatedBy` from a GET are replaced). Stale values are dropped, not refused (ruling R8), so settings saved back unchanged still save: a `hijriFix` year before the current Hijri year, and a dated value ("YYYY-MM-DD", "H-YYYY-MM-DD") before today-30 (the calendar's first day, 14 until ruling R17; a fix for 1448 and dated days saved on 2026-10-02 and sent back on 2027-07-01 with only the weekend changed save without them; 1451 is still `bad_year` then), and `workCalendar` ignores them too (on 2027-06-10 a 1448 `dhulhijja1` fix no longer moves 25 Dhu al-Hijjah off 2027-05-31) | test (`secretary-calendar.test.ts` "rule 474: parseCalendar refuses what the calendar cannot hold", twelve tests) | done |
| 475 | One pure rule decides when the owner may chase someone: `chaseMoment(item, now, cal, away)` in `src/lib/secretary-agenda.ts` (spec §2.1) starts from `chaseAt(item, zone, cal)` (due, else `expectBy`, else creation + 2 working days; after a follow-up the later of that and the follow-up + 2 working days, both counted with `addWorkingDays(…, cal)`, so Eid never counts: a follow-up on Monday 2027-03-08 with Eid 03-09..12 is re-chased Monday 03-15 at the follow-up's time); `why` "away": «أحمد » due Monday 2026-10-05 with «احمد» away until Thursday 10-08 → Sunday 10-11 08:00; away until Sunday 10-04 → Monday 10-05 08:00 in Riyadh, 09:00 in Dubai; the day after on a Ramadan day → its 10:00 window start); then quiet days move the moment to `briefAt` on the next working day (Riyadh Friday → Sunday 08:00, Dubai Saturday or Sunday → Monday 09:00, Thursday and Dubai's Friday not moved, Eid 2027-03-10 → 03-14 08:00, `why` "quiet" with the skipped day's `QuietWhy`, the named holiday when the run held one) and Ramadan's off-hours to the next window start (`outsideRamadanHours`, false on any non-Ramadan day: 20:00 on Monday 2027-02-22 → Tuesday 10:00, or 21:30 the same evening with a second window; 15:00 moves, 10:00 and 14:59 do not; `briefAt` on a working day does not); `why` is the last move's reason, "away" once an absence applied (ruling R5); malformed days throw nothing and move nothing; `partyKey` cleans like `cleanLine`, then drops default-ignorable characters (U+00AD, U+200B-U+200F, U+FE00, U+3164 …), applies NFKC and lower case, strips tashkeel and tatweel, folds أ إ آ ٱ → ا, ة → ه, ى → ي, collapses spaces, and is null past 80 characters (ＤＡＮＡ → dana; | 483 | `GET`, `PUT` and `DELETE /api/secretary/away` (spec §3.2): each handler reads the session first (401 on all three, before any store read), then the role (`read` for GET, `todo.write` for PUT and DELETE: any member), then the rate (`sec:read:{email}` / `sec:write:{email}`, 429 with nothing changed), then the body, of which only `label`, `until`, `note` and `remove` are read (a `scope`, `email`, `by`, `via`, `at` or `ended` there changes nothing; a body that is not an object, or a `remove` that is not a boolean, is 400 `bad_request`); DELETE is idempotent (ruling R21): a second DELETE of the ended entry is 200 with `changed: false`, the entry and the audit as they were, and `{label, remove: true}` deletes it (200 `removed: true`, one `away_end … removed` line); a member's PUT stores `by` = the member's login and `via` "web", and the audit lines name the member; the hash is `sec:away:{sc(u)}`, so a PUT in brand B leaves brand A's hash empty and a DELETE in A of B's contact is 404 with B's entry untouched; GET returns `entries` (`listAway`): running ones first, the soonest back first, then ended ones, the latest first, with `ended: true`, all kept until the clean-up so the owner sees what holds the cap, values that are not entries left out | test (`secretary-away.test.ts` "rule 483: the away route (GET, PUT, DELETE /api/secretary/away): a session, the role, the rate limit, the caller's scope", five tests; the three handlers are declared in `role-gates.test.ts`) | done |

A member's reset, change or removal ends that member's link only. The owner's reset and the founder's reset end the owner's link AND every roster member's (they already sign every member out); the owner's password change ends the owner's only (members keep their sessions and links); a bystander account's link is never touched. A revoke that fails is a 500: the password is already saved and the sessions revoked first, so the user asks for a new reset link (the revoke runs beside `revokeAllMcp` in one `Promise.all`); in `dropMember` it runs after the record is deleted and nothing retries it, the link is refused at read time while the login is gone and, after a re-invite, by the createdAt rule in row 539. The three layers overlap on purpose, so removing one alone is not caught; removing the early revoke and the final one together, or all three, fails. Route-level tests call the real handlers. `tests/helpers/setup.ts` replaces `next/headers` cookies with a jar the test controls and runs `after()` at once; `tests/helpers/session.ts` has `signIn(email)` (a real `session:{token}` record plus its cookie), `signOut()`, `newUser()`, `json()`, `params()` and `settle()`. The memory store honours TTL since 2026-09-18, so expiry rules are testable with fake clocks. Each rule above was checked once by breaking the code and watching the test fail (publish gate, task scope, suspended check, admin check). Since 2026-09-29 `signIn(email, {imp})` also signs in member logins and writes impersonation sessions, and `newMember(owner, role)` builds a member login (password `abc12345`, no seat check) through `createMember`, which no route may call.

Rules 420-434 were added on 2026-10-01 by the launch-security branch (issues #35, #36, #49 in part, #44, #13 and #7), all in `tests/launch-security.test.ts`, with the e2e half of 433-434 in `e2e/auth-flows.spec.ts`: the founder console's server-side key session (420-424), `ADMIN_EMAILS` failing closed (425), Cloudflare Turnstile on signup and forgot-password (426-428), production never on the memory store or the model mock (430-431), no email addresses in the waitlist's logs (432), and the auth forms' error states and accessible password rules (433-434). Rule 429 (key impersonation tied to the key) came with the review fixes, rule 435 (renewing the key session, founder decision 2026-10-01: 15 minutes of inactivity, 12 hours at most) with the idle limit, and rule 436 (the console says when its session ended) with that limit's review fixes; numbers 327-329 of that range are unused. `npm test` after the branch: 95 files, 1511 tests.

Rule 36 was added on 2026-09-20 with the fix it pins, and checked the same way: reverting `listTasks` fails four of its seven tests. It also corrected `task-index.test.ts`, whose 230 all-pending fixture asserted that `listTasks` returned only 200 of them — the defect written down as a pin; those tasks are now created decided, so the test still pins what it was for.

Rule 8 was marked "done" on 2026-09-20 for the first time honestly. The row had always claimed done while its own
text admitted that signup and admin key login were "still manual", and reset was not mentioned at all — three of the
five throttles it names had no test. All three now have one, each asserting the exact attempt at which the limiter
bites; widening any of the three limits fails its test.

Rules 56-63 were added on 2026-09-21 from the P2 and P3 half of the eval audit. Of 39 findings, re-verification
against main found 13 already fixed by the P1 work, 2 not defects and 5 refuted; the 19 confirmed ones reduced to 12
root causes, because the audit had found several of the same bugs from different angles. Each fix was checked by
reverting it (15 reverts, all failing). One defect was found outside the register while doing this: `wipeAccount` had
never deleted the chat feedback log, so a deleted account's typed feedback survived it.

Rules 49-55 were added on 2026-09-21 from an audit of the eval subsystem itself (7 dimensions, 67 agents, 51 of 60
claims confirmed, 12 of them P1). The theme was consistent: the numbers a founder would use to widen auto-approve or
drop an agent were each individually computed and collectively false — an approval rate made of items nobody opened,
thumbs that could never improve, a table covering a different period from the tiles above it. Each rule was checked
by reverting its fix and watching its test fail. Two first attempts at that check did not load (the reverted code
had a syntax error) and one did not fail because the fix has two independent guards; all three were redone properly
before being counted.

Rule 48 was added on 2026-09-20 after CI failed on the pull request. All five session cookies were marked Secure
when `NODE_ENV === "production"`, which describes the build and not the connection. The end-to-end harness serves a
production build over plain http, so WebKit — which, unlike Chromium and unlike WebKit on macOS, does not treat
http://localhost as a trustworthy origin — discarded the cookie, and 40 signed-in route tests were bounced to the
login page on Linux while passing locally. Verified against a running server: loopback http emits no Secure,
`x-forwarded-proto: https` and a real Host both still emit it.

Rules 46-47 were added on 2026-09-20 after the pull request was opened. Rule 46 closed the last finding that had been
left alone for needing sign-off: `registerClient` filtered redirect URIs with `/^https?:\/\//`, so a client could
register `http://evil.example/cb` and be handed an authorization code over plaintext. Rule 47 is the decision that
went with it — a placeholder card is not the artwork that was paid for, so the credit goes back on both paths.

Rules 38-45 were added on 2026-09-20 from the second audit pass. Each was checked by reverting its fix and watching
its test fail: the conversations page 500s again on "?q=a&q=b", the profile reports 2 of 3 tasks, the third charge
goes through after two kills, both trial gates let an expired trial buy an image, and a year-old trial is still owed
its day-one mail. Rule 39 is the one that found a defect rather than pinning one: making the test store copy, as
Upstash does, immediately failed an existing test, and the test was right.

Rule 37 was added on 2026-09-20 for a hydration failure that only Safari saw. `/ar/app/calendar` asked for `ar-SA`,
which WebKit's system ICU resolves to the Umm al-Qura calendar and Node's bundled ICU resolves to the Gregorian one, so
the server sent "20 سبتمبر" and the iPhone rendered "9 ربيع الآخر" above Gregorian day numbers. React threw the whole
tree away (error #418). Two separate causes were confirmed by running the same formatter in both engines: the calendar
divergence above, and the abbreviated Arabic weekday, which is "الأحد" in Node's CLDR and "أحد" in WebKit's whatever
locale is asked for. The first is fixed, the second cannot be — it is data, not configuration — so the three elements
that render `Intl` output inside a client component carry `suppressHydrationWarning`, scoped to their own text.

Rules 27-35 were added on 2026-09-20 with the fixes they pin; each was checked by reverting its fix and watching the test fail (six of them did; the admin-cookie comparison has no observable behaviour difference, so its test guards behaviour only, not the constant-time property).

Next: rule 19 (chat heartbeat) and a cron test with 50 fake users.

Rule 18 was extended on 2026-09-29 to the two public endpoints of the MCP OAuth server. Until then `/api/oauth/token` compared a confidential client's secret with `!==` and neither it nor `/api/oauth/register` had any rate limit, although registration stores a client with no expiry. The secret now goes through `sameSecret`, with a non-string secret from a JSON body treated as wrong (without that, `Buffer.from` would throw and the request would end in a 500), and both routes call `allow()` per IP first. Each change was checked by reverting it and watching `oauth-server.test.ts` fail (four reverts: `!==` back in place, the non-string guard, the token limit and the register limit). The limits are sized for hosted MCP clients that call from shared egress addresses. What the tests cannot show is the constant time itself, so the source check pins the `sameSecret` call instead.

Step 3a of the agent-team upgrade numbered its rules 156-175 on its branch, while main, with teams merged into it first, numbered the teams rules 156-188. When the two merged on 2026-09-29, main's rules kept their numbers, as they did in the earlier merges, and step 3a's moved up by 33: 156-175 became 189-208. Main then numbered phase 1 of Reem, the secretary, 189-212 the same day, and `tests/secretary.test.ts`, `tests/secretary-brief.test.ts` and `tests/secretary-page.test.ts` name those numbers, so when main's Reem change (#225) was merged into step 3a, main's rules kept their numbers again and step 3a's moved up by 24 more: 189-208 became 213-232 (156-175 on the branch). Main then numbered its security gates from the Taskuary study (#227) 213-220, and `tests/security-gates.test.ts` names those numbers, so when #227 was merged into step 3a after main's security gates, main's rules kept their numbers again and step 3a's moved up by 8 more: 213-232 became 221-240. Main then numbered pricing phase 1 (#228) 241-250, leaving 221-240 to step 3a, so when #228 was merged into step 3a on 2026-09-30 the rule the review of the #227 merge had added as 241 was folded into rule 240 (the account wipe), since 251-260 are #213's. The tests' `describe` names and comments follow the new numbers. For the same reason step 3a's sections of `flows.md` are 30-32 (25-27 on its branch, 26-28 after the teams merge, 27-29 after the Reem merge, 29-31 after the security gates merge), since Reem's section is 25, the teams section 26, #227's sections 27 (brand proposals) and 28 (the judge withdraws an auto-approval) and #228's section 29 (brands: unlimited, rate-limited, locked), and its three routes are rows 104-106 of `permissions.md` (101-103 before), since Reem's routes hold 101-103; #227's route is the lettered row 9a and #228 added no row, so no row moved. What merging #227 changed in step 3a: its per-site changes to the six generation paths (the item's channel reaching `initialStatus`) live once in `taskFromItem` (rules 213 and 223); chat `publish_item` now goes through `publishOnce` and needs a person's decision, and records its `published` row only after a publish that went out (rule 233); `update_brand` records `brand_changed` only when the brand file changed, and `POST /api/brand/proposals/{id}` records one when a proposal is applied (rule 233); the judge's withdrawal of an auto-approval sits beside its activity rows (rules 217 and 232). The review of that merge (2026-09-30) added a rule that a deleted account's brand proposals, #227's records, go with it (241 at first, now part of rule 240), pinned rule 213 through `generateBatch` itself (one batch's WhatsApp and Instagram items with the same `{{1}}` body, rules 213 and 223), and made the publish route record a post an `earlier_live` answer proves went out: its `published` row and, for a pending item, `decidedAt` and the `publish` decision at the earlier attempt's time (rule 233). What merging #228 changed in step 3a: its task-cost rises (weekly plan 2, weekly report 3, follow-up cadence 4) reach every path through `TASK_COST`, which `generateBatch` charges; its code check on the chat's `draft_items` types (rule 250) runs before `generateBatch`, so a refused type reaches no charge and no activity row; the floor test's check that a draft is charged by the estimate's formula reads `countCost` in `src/lib/generate-batch.ts`, where the chat's charge now lives (rule 241); the Sunday plan's per-brand default (rule 247) holds for `runWeekly`, which reads `getSchedule` of the caller's scope before it calls `generateBatch`; `generateBatch` itself now throws `not_draft_type` for a small ask of a type outside `DRAFT_TYPES` (rule 250); and `activity-sources.test.ts` drafts a WhatsApp item (Noor's) in a turn with Majed instead of a sales script, which `draft_items` now refuses (rule 232). The three paragraphs below give the earlier numbers too. What the teams merge changed in step 3a's rules and tests: `byOf` reads only `u.actor` (rule 225), so its tests prove support access from a session carrying `imp` (recorded with the founder's own email, where the branch recorded `"founder"`) and show the impersonation cookies alone change nothing; a user with no actor is recorded as the system, so the tests that stood in a bare `getUser` record for a request (`activity.test.ts`, `activity-sources.test.ts`'s support-access chat turn, `chat-deadline.test.ts`) now sign in and use what `currentUser` returns, which teams' `runTool` gate needs too (rule 175); and the three routes step 3a added got teams' gates, `governance.write` for a restore and `read` for the activity list and export (rules 171, 227 and 237, with tests in `role-gates.test.ts` and `governance-history.test.ts`).

Rules 261-295 (233-267 on the step 3b branch; 251-260 are the homepage rules of PR #213) were added on 2026-09-30 with step 3b of the agent-team upgrade (PR #230): 261-270 the cross-agent review (`tests/cross-review.test.ts`), 271-282 Majed's handoffs (`tests/handoffs.test.ts`), 283-292 Majed's opt-in auto-run (`tests/handoff-auto.test.ts`; the late brand of rule 291 also in `tests/cron-batch.test.ts`) and 293-295 its review fixes (a flagged item's chat card, the handoff card's re-reads, the Governance page's queue); 296 (Majed's auto-run keeps the margin floor on a cold cache, `tests/handoff-auto.test.ts` and `tests/pricing-floor.test.ts`) was added by the review of the merge below. When step 3a, with main's security gates (#227), pricing phase 1 (#228) and docs fixes (#229), was merged into step 3b on 2026-09-30, every citation of these rules moved by 28 and step 3b's citations of step 3a's rules by 8 (213-232 to 221-240), each by where the line came from; its `flows.md` sections moved from 30-32 to 33-35.

Rules 232-240 (167-175 on the step 3 branch, 200-208 until main's Reem change was merged in, 224-232 before main's security gates (#227) were merged in) were added on 2026-09-29 with step 3 of the agent-team upgrade (part L, the per-agent activity log). Until then nothing said what each agent did in a brand or what happened to its work: `track()` keeps only global and per-account counters, `admin:log` only founder actions, and `decisions:{scope}` only the owner's decisions, so an owner could not see which agent spent what, a routine's quiet days, a render refunded overnight, or a publish from chat. The log follows the decision records' pattern (one JSON row per action, `pushCapped` per scope, a tolerant sorted read, deleted by `wipeAccount`), records one row per batch, never per item, and never fails the action it describes. Each rule was checked by reverting its fix and watching its test fail (39 reverts: the batch row, the refusal rows, the scheduled refusal recorded twice, `by` from the caller, the judge's rows, the autofail rows, the judge's via, the decision rows, a publish as a decision row, the route's and the chat tool's publish rows, the brand value in the note, chat not passing its turn, MCP's token, the external draft row, the restore's kind, the once-a-period marker, a retry recorded, the routine's `error`, Run now's owner, the render's start row and pre-check rows, the job's stored agent and scope, the wiped-account check, the recorder's catch, the note's bound, the sort, the `by` check, the page cut inside one time, the route's agent check, rate limit and scope, the CSV's `csvSafe` and BOM, the page's server data, the founder console's rows, and each of the two wipe keys). Decided rather than specified: a publish is recorded by the publish paths, not by `recordDecisions`, because a publish of an already approved item is no decision but is still a publish; `write_file` keeps the file author `noor` (the Files page's author label has two values, Noor and the owner) and the row names the talking agent; the refusal rows say what the batch would have cost in `credits`; a routine success is two rows (`generated` and `routine_ran`); the media rows carry `media` and `refunded` besides the fields the design listed.

Rules 225-231 (160-166 on the step 3 branch, 193-199 until main's Reem change was merged in, 217-223 before main's security gates (#227) were merged in) were added on 2026-09-29 with step 3 of the agent-team upgrade (part H: who acted, governance history with restore, prompt-version stamps). Until then a governance save overwrote the settings with no lock, no history and no record of who made it, so an owner could not see or undo a change (an auto-approve switched on, a cap removed), two saves could lose one another, and a founder's change through support access read as the owner's; decisions on items did not say who decided; and nothing recorded which prompt produced an item or a golden score, so a drop could not be told apart from a prompt change. `byOf` (`src/lib/actor.ts`) is the single place that says who acted, written so PR #223's `u.actor` could replace its cookie fallback in one file; at the merge with teams it did: `byOf` now reads only `u.actor` (typed with `auth.ts`'s `Actor`), a founder's support access is proven by the session record's `imp`, the impersonation cookies are never read, and a user with no actor (a cron or script, never a request) is the system, not the owner. A restore sends a full patch because `saveGov` keeps what a partial patch does not name. Each rule was checked by reverting its fix and watching its test fail (26 reverts: the impersonation cookie, `actor.email` for a founder, a member's own email, `by` on each of the two decision routes, `by` taken from the body, the lock, a revision on a no-op, `govDiff`'s off-equals-unset, the snapshot restored as it is, the current value for a missing setting, a restore that skips the checks, `rollbackOf`, the rate limit, the id check in the route and before the read, a stored time that does not parse, the page's history, the wipe keys, `pb` in `taskFromItem`, a hash not taken from the content, review.md in the code hash, `promptChanged` without the previous hashes, carried rows, the regression tag, and the run's `playbooks`). Decided rather than specified: the stamp lives in its own module (`prompt-stamp.ts`), since putting it in `skills.ts` would import `noor.ts` and `judge.ts` into a module both of them import; the code hash covers the reviewer's checks and the judge's rubric without review.md (`reviewerRubric`, `JUDGE_RUBRIC`, both split out with no change to either prompt), so each source has exactly one hash; the industry part is `general:<hash>` when no industry playbook matches; the routes answer with the change as the history lists it (`entry`) rather than the whole revision.

Rules 221-224 (156-159 on the step 3 branch, 189-192 until main's Reem change was merged in, 213-216 before main's security gates (#227) were merged in) were added on 2026-09-29 with step 3 of the agent-team upgrade (part G, one generation pipeline). Until then the six paths that make a paid text batch (the generate route, the two chat tools, MCP `generate_content`, the weekly plan and the routines) each repeated the checks, the charge, the refund, the task construction, the save and the judge call, and five of them saved their tasks with an `await` inside a loop; a rule added to one path (the agent's cap, the refund receipt, the judge's grounding) had to be copied into the others by hand, and rule 95 named the ones no test would catch dropping it. `generateBatch` (`src/lib/generate-batch.ts`) now does all of it in one order, with no behaviour change beyond the saves running together and one creation time per batch; the callers keep their own answers, tracking and limits. Each rule was checked by reverting its fix and watching its test fail (17 reverts: the paused check, the paused check reaching chat, brand before trial, the data check, the settle on a refusal and on the refund, the refund's receipt, the reads inside the try, the forced pending, the concurrent save, the judge's grounding and its count, the one creation time, the brief override, the weekly plan's `via`, MCP's `source`, and the route's scope). Decided rather than specified: chat keeps no paused pre-check (rule 76's test pins its charge-and-refund backstop); the routine's attempt is given back through a `settle` callback rather than a hold key, because the counter is restored to a computed value with its TTL; a routine whose cart data disappears between its gate and the charge answers `no_data` (not pinned: the window is a few milliseconds).

Rules 144-148 were added on 2026-09-29 with MCP `save_draft`, so an owner can write with their own Claude or ChatGPT subscription and still review, approve and publish in Nashr. Until then the owner's assistant could read the brand rules and check a draft for free, but the only way into the inbox was `generate_content` or `ask_noor`, which spend Nashr's credits and models. Each rule was checked by reverting its fix and watching its test fail (five reverts: the rate check, `initialStatus(false, ...)`, `sc(u)` replaced by the email, the `external` source, and the body length check). Decided rather than specified: a draft that breaks a brand rule is saved held rather than refused, like every other creation path, so the owner can fix it rather than lose it; the tool is not trial-gated, like `check_brand`, because it spends nothing; `source` gets its own value `external` instead of `chat`, so reports can tell the owner's own assistant from Noor, and Dana's routine gate counts an external draft as the owner's activity.

Rules 241-250 were added on 2026-09-30 with pricing phase 1 (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-pricing-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-29-pricing.md`): one plan table in `src/lib/pricing.ts` (299 / 499 / 999 SAR, 300 / 700 / 1,500 credits, 1 / 3 / 7 seats, Agency by contract), unlimited brands with a 9-credit setup for each added brand, the Sunday plan on by default for the default brand only, and the 60% margin floor as a test. The same change rewrote rules 14, 28, 42, 179 and 180: the first brand's onboarding stays free while an added one's costs 9, a second brand no longer needs a larger plan, and the seat numbers moved from Agency to Business. Founder decisions pinned by these tests (2026-09-29): the weekly report at 3 credits and the cron-driven types checked on a cold cache, the one-item chat draft kept at 1 credit as a named exemption with a cap, and «الوكالات» as the Arabic name of the custom tier. The rules were numbered 189-198 on the branch and moved up by 24 before the merge, because main's secretary change (#225) took 189-212 first. A verified review of the branch the same day changed rules 244-248: the added brand's setup charge moved from its own key (`onboardpaid:{scope}`, one day) to the account record, written and dropped in the same save as the debit and the refund, because a refund and a marker in two records could refund twice or build free (a slow run whose 240 s lock expired, or a save that failed after it was written); the run lock became 330 s with an owner-token release; the daily cron refunds a hold no run used for a day; a stored `weeklyPlan` on an added brand counts only with its stamp; clearing a deal clears its seats; a brand-add refusal says which window; the copy says only the build from a URL is charged; and the Updates entry names the task-price rises.

Rules 440-452 were added on 2026-10-01 with credit top-ups (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-pricing-design.md` section 6 rule 5; founder decisions of 2026-10-01: packs of 100 / 300 / 1,000 at 1 SAR a credit plus VAT, valid 90 days from activation, spent after the plan's monthly credits, sold to paid plans only, activated by hand in the founder console while there is no payment gateway; `flows.md` 38). `User.credits` stays the whole balance, so the readers that show or check it did not change; the packs are lots on the account record (`src/lib/topups.ts`), and every writer of the balance now goes through the pack-aware helpers. Two choices were the safe ones and were pinned: a refund owed to a pack that has expired or was voided is dropped rather than moved into the plan credits (the founder chose otherwise on 2026-10-02: rule 461 moves it into the plan credits), and an agent's cap still counts every credit the agent spends, from a pack or not. Numbering: 440-459 were reserved for this change. Rule 453 came with the review of 2026-10-01 (the profile's packs). Rules 454-459 came on 2026-10-02, after main (#232 to #238) was merged into the branch: a pack is a customer payment, so "Add top-up" records it through the partner programme's `recordPayment` and `settle` and a referred customer's top-up earns the partner's 15% exactly once (454), voiding the pack voids that payment and its commission (455); and the review findings on PR #239: the profile's split of plan and top-up credits (456), the out-of-credits lines by plan and role (457), main's paid-once Sunday holds carrying the top-up receipt (458) and the payment date as the Riyadh day (459). The one entry point for a top-up's payment is the console's "Add top-up": the Payments panel records plan payments only and says so. Rules 460-464 came on 2026-10-02, after PR #239 was merged, with the founder's answers to its four open questions; 440-459 were full, so they take the next block (460-479), which main did not use (no row above 459) and no open pull request had reserved. Answers 1 and 2 changed the code: a pack's unused credits can be refunded before it expires, at the price per credit paid and excluding VAT, with the console's "Refund unused" (460 the amount and the ledger, 462 the payment and the commission cut by commission × refunded / paid, 463 the route and the console, 464 what the owner is told), and a failed charge's credits owed to a pack that is gone or refunded go to the plan credits (461, replacing the "dropped" choice above and in rules 442 and 458). Answers 3 and 4 kept the code as it was: top-up revenue is counted by the payment date the founder enters (448), and a pack's payment starts the partner's 30 days and 4 months while no plan payment stands, moving to the plan payment once one is recorded (454).

Rules 308-316 were added on 2026-10-01 with pricing phase 2 (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-pricing-design.md` section 6 rules 1 and 2, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-29-pricing.md` phase 2): the monthly chat allowance per account (308-310), the hard per-message guards checked in code before every model call (311 the $0.35 ceiling, 312 the history guard), chat against the 60% margin floor (313), the counters going with the account (314), what the owner sees (315) and the founder console's chat column and override (316). Until then chat was free in credits and bounded only by the hourly and daily rate limits, a turn could make five calls of 8,000 output tokens (over $1), and the whole thread up to 16 messages went to the model however long it was. Founder decisions pinned by these tests (2026-10-01): when the messages run out chat pauses until the 1st (UTC), unless the owner of a paid plan turns "continue on credits" on, which bills each extra message its metered cost at the floor rate (refining the spec's "until the next month or a top-up": top-ups are not built); and one message may cost at most $0.35 in model calls, which sets `chatOverflowHold()` at 5 credits: the least the balance must hold for a message on credits and the most one is billed. The chat model stays Opus: the Sonnet switch waits for the golden eval and the Gulf-dialect check. Rule 248's copy checks (`pricing-copy.test.ts`) now also cover the cards' chat lines, the credits table's chat row ("Included" / «ضمن خطتك», up to the plan's monthly messages, the 5-credit bound from `chatOverflowHold()`), the trial line, the cost FAQ, the help answer and the Updates entry, and its scan for numbers typed into copy bans 250, 650 and 1,300; `content-claims.test.ts` bans copy that calls chat free or unlimited. Rule 307 was the Jev evaluation (#219, merged the same day; removed by #238, see its row). A review of the build the same day changed rules 309-316: the first build took the 5 credits before a message on credits and gave back what it did not use, which let the hold eat the balance the turn's own tools were about to spend, so nothing is taken before the turn any more (the gate only checks the balance) and the metered cost is billed after the reply, at most 5 credits and at most what the balance still holds, into a new counter `usage:{email}:{YYYY-MM}:chat_cred`; `ChatStatus` gained `counted` (the raw count, which can pass a lowered allowance) and `overflow.credits`; the budget guard gained `fitFirstCall` (`CHAT_FIRST_MIN_OUT` 4,000, with `CHAT_MIN_OUT` at 2,048) and the cache-aware input estimate; a call ended by `max_tokens` is no longer acted on; leading assistant messages are kept; the reply no longer carries `usd`; the copy lost every held, comes-back and "usually under one credit" sentence (an Opus turn is estimated near $0.08, above one credit's $0.071 at the floor rate), and `content-claims.test.ts` bans them in both languages; the owner sees paused for a low balance and chat off; and the founder console divides chat cost by every message sent.

The teams rules were numbered 116-148 on their branch, while main numbered the media rules of the same day 116-143. When the two merged on 2026-09-29, main's rules kept their numbers, as they did when the media rules were merged, and the teams rules moved up by 28: 116-148 became 144-176. Main then numbered MCP `save_draft` and the updates and wishlist page 144-155 the same day, and `tests/mcp-save-draft.test.ts`, `tests/wishlist.test.ts` and `tests/updates-page.test.ts` name those numbers, so when teams merged with them main's rules kept their numbers again and the teams rules moved up by 12 more: 144-176 became 156-188 (116-148 on the teams branch). For the same reason the teams section of `flows.md` was 25, since main's updates and wishlist section is 24; it is 26 since main's Reem change put its own section at 25. The two paragraphs below give both numbers.

Rules 182-188 (142-148 on the teams branch) were added on 2026-09-29 from a verified review of the teams build (a completeness critic, then an independent skeptic per finding). Three fixes were checked by reverting them one at a time and watching their test fail: removal without `lock:member` (the removed login came back), accept without the index re-check (a cancelled invite still joined), and `enforceSeats` ranking the whole team (re-applying Pro swapped two members). The rest are pinned by direct assertions on the refusal or the absence of the data. Decided rather than specified: a failed invite email withdraws the invite instead of handing the link over (the spec's "otherwise return it once" now means only "mail not set up"); performance import became admin+ like its delete, since an upload replaces the last one; a hand-joined member does not free its address at signup, because signup verifies nothing either and that would let anyone who knows a member's address evict them, so the founder console's removal is the remedy.

Rules 156-180 (116-140 on the teams branch) were added on 2026-09-29 with teams: member logins, invites, seats and one role table (`src/lib/roles.ts`, `permissions.md` 0.1, `flows.md` 26). Until then there was no membership or role model: the owner was the only person who could sign in to an account. The account stays the owner's record so that no tenant or credit call site changed; what is new is who is acting (`actor`), and every rule above is about keeping that separate from the account: a member's password, sessions and reset links are its own, a founder is recognised by the actor and not by the account's email, and a request with no actor is refused. `tests/role-gates.test.ts` is the fail-closed half: a new mutating route that nobody has given a role fails the suite until someone decides who may call it. Checked by breaking the code and watching tests fail: ten rules one at a time against `teams-core.test.ts` (the founder check by `u.email`, a member's scope from its own email, the `acceptedAt` check, the impersonation block, the bearer's member refusal, the `wipeTeam` cascade, the seat re-check at accept, `teamSessionsAfter`, `saveUser`'s actor strip, and the `inviteuse:` lock, which no test caught until a direct assertion was added), and the governance `forbid` and the `runTool` gate against `role-gates.test.ts` (five failures). Four existing tests changed with it: `revocation.test.ts` removes `sessionsAfter` to emulate an account from before 2026-09-18, since `createUser` now stamps it, and `regressions.test.ts`, `brand-guard.test.ts` and `routes-onboard-publish.test.ts` call `runTool` with a signed-in owner, since a user without an actor is now refused.

The media rules were numbered 92-119 on their branch, while main numbered its rules of the same day 92-115 (steps 1 and 2 of the agent-team upgrade and the shared-link onboarding fix). When the two merged on 2026-09-29, main's rules kept their numbers, because its tests, `e2e/auth-flows.spec.ts`, `docs/Playbook_Sources.md` and `docs/Owner_Tasks_AR.md` name them, and the media rules moved up by 24: 92-111 became 116-135 and 112-119 became 136-143. The media spec and plan under `docs/superpowers/` still use the branch numbers.

Rules 136-143 were added on 2026-09-29 from the verified review of the media change (and rules 4, 5, 47, 122, 125, 129 and 134 extended). `task-writes.test.ts`, `rate-window.test.ts` and `chat-deadline.test.ts` are the review's new files, and so is `media-controls.test.ts` (the inbox's media controls rendered to markup, and their two timer chains), which pins rule 143, the inbox side of rule 142 and the pause note of rule 125 and was missing from this map until the doc check of the same day; `npx vitest run` then counted 57 files and 713 tests (the header said 53 and 655 before the review). The tests of rules 136-141 were each checked by putting the old behaviour back in a copy of the code and watching them fail: the old whole-task writers in the publish route, the chat tool, the approve route and the engine; the parallel done-and-task save; the refund taken as made when its marker exists; the first-hit-only SET in `allow()`; the unlock that threw after a saved balance change; the sweep that skipped the copy gap; the unguarded analytics call; and the chat tool's fixed 100 s deadline and unconditional "credit was returned". Rule 142 pins the placeholder fix of the same review (`placeholder` in `src/lib/media/start.ts` now writes through `updateTask` and refuses over artwork); no revert of it, or of rule 143, is recorded.

Rules 116-135 were added on 2026-09-29 with kie.ai, Higgsfield, Vercel Blob and video (design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-media-providers-design.md`). The same change rewrote rules 40, 44, 47, 75 and 76: the placeholder card is no longer charged and refunded but not charged at all, the trial check and the artwork test (`hasArtwork`) are shared by every media path, and the pause now means fixed prompts, not a paused renderer. Gaps left open and named in their rows: the video route's `trial_over` and the key-in-URL case on `GET /api/admin/media`. The third, the inbox's pause note, is rendered by `media-controls.test.ts` since the review (rule 125).

Rules 189-207 (156-174 before main's teams rules took those numbers) were added on 2026-09-29 with phase 1 of Reem, the secretary agent (spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-secretary-reem-phase1.md`): the owner's own to-dos and follow-ups per brand, a computed agenda in Riyadh time, `/api/secretary`, four free chat and MCP tools, the My to-dos page, the brief block and the overdue follow-up. Five of them were checked by reverting their rule and watching the test fail: the owner-only check in `changeItem` and `mutateItem` (rule 191), the `rev` check (194), the Riyadh offset in `riyadhDay` (197), the MGET in `listOpenItems` replaced by one read per item (198), and the "important" email clause (199). The same change extended rules 93 and 96 (the reviewer prompts are distinct among the agents that own a type; eight playbooks), the golden set's agent coverage (agents that own a type), `client-bundle.test.ts` (`@/lib/secretary` is server only; `secretary-agenda.ts` imports only `./dates` and `./agents`) and `scope-usage.test.ts` (the secretary readers). A review of the change the same day found that an MCP client could close any item by moving its link onto an approved inbox item (`todo_update {id, task_id}`, then `agenda_get`), since the MCP route does not check arguments against the schema; that expired deleted items in archived brands and suspended accounts were never purged; that a proposal on a snoozed item stayed hidden; that a restore skipped the open cap; that agents were never told today's date; that the copy promised a reminder at the due time; and that several rules were stated but not pinned (a scope in a PATCH body or tool arguments, the cron's purge, the linked-task MGET, private notes, the next-day follow-up, the read limit, the acting agent in the audit). Rules 190-193, 195, 198, 200 and 202-204 were extended and 205-207 added; each fix was checked by reverting it and watching its test fail (22 reverts: the three link checks, the snooze clear on a proposal and the classify order, the restore cap, the private-note filter and both owner guards, the scope from the tool arguments and from the PATCH body, the read limit, the hard-coded chat actor, the linked-task MGET, the NOW line, the past-date notice, the cron's `secretaryDaily` call and its idle scopes, the follow-up fingerprint, the Arabic dual, the governance cap list and the notes on the page).

Rules 208-211 (175-178 on the branch) were added on 2026-09-29 with five fixes from a line-by-line comparison of phase 1 with the Taskuary repository (MIT; ideas only, no code copied), and rules 197, 200, 204, 206 and 207 were rewritten with them. The overdue follow-up was one per brand per day that repeated its text every day and said nothing of an item that fell behind later; it is now one alert per item on days 0, 1, 3 and 7 (rule 200). The notification marker was read and then written, so two overlapping runs could both notify; it is claimed with SET NX (rule 208). An undated debt sat in "waiting" forever; the chase clock moves it to "time to follow up" after two working days (rule 209). Every date assumed Riyadh; each brand now keeps one of six Gulf zones (rule 210). The confirm card showed an agent's words as if they were the evidence; it now labels them a claim next to what code checked (rule 211). Each rule was checked by reverting its fix and watching its test fail (21 reverts; the one that first passed, dropping the day-7 bound, led to running rule 200's clock to day 14). Decided rather than specified: the alert steps count local days from the due date, not from the first run that saw the item late, so the day-0 alert fires only for an item already late at the 08:00 run of its due day; the UAE weekend is Saturday and Sunday; an agent records a follow-up only as the owner's statement, and nothing is ever sent; the cold bucket covers the owner's own undated items only. Rule 212 was added when main's teams change was merged into the branch the same day: Reem's routes, tools and page follow the role table (members write, admins close).

Rules 149-155 were added on 2026-09-29 with the public Updates & wishlist page (`/{lang}/updates`): a changelog of merged work and a wishlist signed-in owners vote on, with suggestions going to a founder-only queue. The body-identity test was checked by reverting its rule (the vote route taking `email` from the body) and watching it fail.

Rules 111-115 were added on 2026-09-29 with step 2 of the agent-team upgrade (part A, routines for more agents). Until then Noor's Sunday plan was the only work any agent did on its own; Dana, Razan and Majed only ever answered a request. A routine is opt-in per brand, paid like a batch the owner asks for, and never runs on nothing: each gate is code that reads the workspace's own data, not a prompt. Each rule was checked by reverting its fix and watching its test fail (26 reverts: the paused, no-key and trial checks, the period check under the lock (the one before it is a cheap early exit and alone is not pinned), the gate call, each gate's conditions (Dana's exclusion of scheduled items, her decision-record and ad-sync signals, Noor's "synced since the last run", Razan's 35 days, Majed's light-index union and distinct items), the batch-size weekly cap, the agent's cap, the refund and its receipt, the data block passed to generation, `source:"scheduled"`, `routinelast`, the cron's due filter and its bearer check, the PUT's id check, the per-id merge, the two `statsFor` branches, and the wipe). Decided rather than specified: Dana's gate leaves out items the weekly plan or a routine created on their own, since otherwise the routines would keep each other busy for an absent owner; Majed's gate counts different items rather than records, and reads the light index's `decidedAt` next to the decision records, which are new; the last run is read again under the lock, because a chain hop can start an item another hop is finishing. The in-app Routines card has no E2E test yet. Review fixes the same day: the weekly cap was read once per routine before a generation of 30-120 s while the cron runs a brand's routines side by side, so two routines could each pass on the same count (now the hold under `routinecap:`); the cron's attempt count is per day, so a run killed after its charge could be charged again every day of its week or month (now `routinetry:` per period); the long-lived `notifseen` markers that name a brand outlived a deleted account; and the Routines card promised runs "only when there is something new", which only Noor's gate checks.

Rules 106-110 were added on 2026-09-29 with step 2 of the agent-team upgrade (part B, per-agent usage and monthly credit caps). Until then the ledger recorded nothing but the balance, so neither the owner nor the founder could say which agent spent what, and nothing but the balance bounded one agent's work. The counters live on the User record rather than in their own keys so that the debit and the count are one save under the lock every balance change already takes; a separate `INCRBY` next to `saveUser` could drift from the balance on a crash and let parallel charges pass a cap. Each rule was checked by reverting its fix and watching its test fail (21 reverts: the cap check in `charge`, the refund's month check, the refund's counter decrement, the cap on the generate route, MCP, chat `generate_image` and the weekly plan, the receipt on the generate route's and the image route's refunds, the image's owning agent, the chat `limit:agent_cap` action, the warning's fingerprint, the NaN and auto-approve checks in `saveGov`, the agent counters in `usageMonthKeys`, the hand-written wipe list, and the agent on each of the judge, image-prompt, onboarding, chat and review meters). Two things were decided rather than specified: MCP answers the cap with a tool error in its JSON-RPC result, like its insufficient-credits answer, because an HTTP 402 would break the protocol for the client; and the whole-record user saves outside the lock (`permissions.md` 5.18) can now also overwrite the counters, which is the same known defect as for the balance.

Rules 102-105 were added on 2026-09-29 with step 2 of the agent-team upgrade (part D, feedback into the owning agent's prompts). Until then the only thing an agent learned from the owner was the list of preference notes, which went to every agent alike, so a note about Noor's Instagram captions steered Razan's pricing review; judge notes and chat thumbs-down reasons were stored and never read. The feedback is gated by `gov.learning`, framed as data and kept out of the cached blocks, because judge notes are model output that may quote crawled text and every decision would otherwise rewrite the cache. Each rule was checked by reverting its fix and watching its test fail (ten reverts: the feedback dropped from the generate route, MCP and the weekly plan; the chat thumbs switch; the chat turn's per-agent preferences; the learning gate; the thumbs agent filter; the second escape at the frame; the block moved into the cached workspace block; and the golden set given feedback). Left as they were: preference notes are still inserted raw under "highest priority", `preferences.md` still reaches every agent through the memory files, and `/api/chat/feedback` has no rate limit although its reasons now reach the chat prompt (5 newest, 200 characters).

Rules 98-101 were added on 2026-09-29 with step 2 of the agent-team upgrade (part C, decision records). Until then an owner's decision survived only as the task's current `status`, `editedBody` and `decidedAt`, each overwritten by the next save (and `decidedAt` cleared on a reopen), and a rejection's reason was kept only as a preference note when learning was on, so nothing could say what the owner did to an agent's work, in which order, or why. Records are append-only and written at decision time, never rebuilt from the task, because the judge reloads and re-saves tasks without a lock. Each rule was checked by reverting its fix and watching its test fail (four reverts: the wipe line, the publish record, `source: "manual"` on the route, and the re-saved-edit check). One defect was noticed and left alone: an edit that restores the original text is not saved, because the route only stores `editedBody` when it differs from `body`, so the earlier edit stays.

Rules 92-96 were added on 2026-09-29 with step 1 of the agent-team upgrade (ideas from the paperclip and agency-agents repositories; sources in `docs/Playbook_Sources.md`). The onboarding goal had only ever reached the trial activation bar; the reviewer introduced itself as Noor's for every agent's work; and the judge saw neither the owner's notes nor the data a report was built from, so an automatic fail for invented numbers would have failed real ones. An adversarial review of the first version found that hoisting the data read out of the refunding `try` lost the credits on a failed read after the charge (fixed, and pinned by the refund test), and that the reviewer and judge still lacked the memory files. Each rule was checked by reverting its fix and watching its test fail (ten reverts). The golden set moved to version 5 because the judge's inputs and scoring changed; a before/after run waits until Claude is switched back on in production.

Rules 90-91 were added on 2026-09-28 with the ad codes. Building them surfaced a real defect: a code's digits made the "contains a number or price" pattern track Nashr's own ads instead of the wording, which would have taught Noor a false lesson the more owners used codes.

Rules 87-89 were added on 2026-09-28 with the Meta Ads and Snapchat Ads readers, which turn the CSV import's one-off upload into a daily read of the owner's own ad accounts.

Rules 82-84 were added on 2026-09-28 with the campaign CSV import, the small-business form of a closed loop: until the ad connectors report results, an export the owner uploads is how Noor learns what worked.

Rules 77-80 were added on 2026-09-28 with the brand gate, the MCP brand tools and the launch journey. Until then the banned words reached the model as a prompt line, the reviewer as a checklist item and the judge as a score, and nothing in code stopped a draft containing one from being approved, auto-approved or published.

Rule 64 was added on 2026-09-22 with the honest-claims change. Until then the claims test read `content/blog` only, while the homepage log showed invented results (a revenue figure, a customer count, an acceptance rate) and the plan cards sold features that are not built. The sweep was checked by running it against main before the fix: every one of its 21 patterns matched at least once (78 hits), and none match after it. The review of 27 Sep widened the patterns to the Arabic wordings and the other word forms of the same removed claims. Four more were added the same day for the follow-ups outside that change (the free-tool pages' daily-automation line, the chat prompt's "no auto-publishing" on trial, the inspiration cards' unsourced results, and the unsourced superlatives in the season and industry prompts); on main before the fix they matched 9 lines, and none after it. The affiliate terms, the first-100-brands offer and the early-partner discount are owner decisions and are not in the list. `content-claims.test.ts` walks `src/app`, `src/components`, `src/lib` and `content/blog` by directory listing, so it already sweeps the new homepage's copy (`src/components/home/`, `src/lib/home.ts`, `src/lib/dictionary.ts`) with no change to the test itself.

Rules 251-257, 259 and 260 were added on 2026-09-28 with the new homepage (`src/app/[lang]/page.tsx`: Story, Lineup, Dictionary, Truths, Pricing, Faq), replacing `src/components/Landing.tsx` and `Sections.tsx`. The questions, banter and truths are pinned against the real agent roster and its tasks; the dictionary game's reducer and its two generated samples are pinned as pure functions, and its silence to the network is pinned end-to-end; the lineup's flip and banter timing are pinned as pure functions in `src/lib/flip-clock.ts` and `src/lib/banter-clock.ts` so their exact numbers (300 ms stagger, 2400 ms flip, 1500/2200/8000/2200 ms banter) cannot drift silently; and the accessibility, responsive and reduced-motion behaviour is pinned end-to-end. Two things stay short of a full pin: the Story's hand-off of a resumed question to `QuestionFlip` is checked only at the pure-function level, not by rendering the Story and reading a lineup figure's text; and WebKit's blink and breathing are not asserted, because the pointer test that exercises them is skipped on the suite's only WebKit project (mobile Safari, which has no pointer to follow). Rule 258 is not new with the homepage: `client-bundle.test.ts` already checked every client component against every server-only module and did not change in this branch. Its row was missing from this map and was added at the same time, because `src/components/home/` adds a dozen new client components for it to scan. The scan finds client components by their `"use client"` line, so `Figure.tsx`, which uses state and had no directive, now carries one to stay inside it. Rule 259 extends the lineup's pause button of rule 257 to the whole page after the final review of the branch: the storm, the scroll cue and the story's flip ran forever with no way to stop them except a button nine screens down. Rule 260 came from the same review.

Rules 251, 255 and 257 changed on 2026-09-29 when Reem, the secretary, joined the homepage as its eighth agent (her copy and drawing are drafts pending the founder's approval, spec §5.2). She owns no task type, so her questions are backed by the `todo` feature instead; `home-copy.test.ts` keeps its own map from each feature to the agent capability that runs it and the page that shows it, so the copy cannot vouch for itself. The old 343 ms stagger was 2400 ms divided by seven: with an eighth agent the last one wrapped round to 1 ms after Noor, so the stagger is now 300 ms and `flip-clock.test.ts` checks it against the cast. The story's height, its finale chapter, the owner's solo position on phones and the storm's ring size now follow the cast instead of the numbers 900svh, 8 and 36.

Rules 251 and 257 changed again on 2026-09-30 with review fixes to Reem. Her draft colour moved from amber `#f4b942` to soft coral `#ffa38a`: the amber was all but the site's accent `#F2B33D`, the colour of the CTA and of focus rings. Her question «مين باقي يدين لي؟» ("Who still owes me?") became «مين ما رد علي للحين؟» ("Who still hasn't got back to me?"). `home-copy.test.ts` now keeps her copy from promising reminders or channels that do not exist yet, and was watched failing on a value line that promised a WhatsApp reminder before it passed on the real copy. `e2e/home.spec.ts` counts the figures from the cast instead of a fixed 9, presses Reem to check that an agent with no banter line answers with its value line, and checks that the lineup fits at 1280 px: its columns' minimum width went from 132 px to 120 px, since nine columns at 132 px needed 1363 px and scrolled the strip on a 1280 px screen. Her turned pose no longer mirrors her planner away from the pencil in her hand (`propStays` in `Doodle.tsx`); no test pins the drawing.

Rules 350-367 were added on 2026-10-01 on branch `claude/launch-mail-origin` (launch mail and domain readiness): one-time emails marked as sent only after Resend accepted them (350-352, #11, `mail-once.test.ts`), the founder console's Leads tab (353-355, #134, `leads.test.ts`), one canonical app origin (356-358, #8 and #169, `origin.test.ts`) and share cards (359-362, #45, `share-meta.test.ts` and `e2e/public-pages.spec.ts`); the founder's decisions of 2026-10-01 added the one-time repair of nudge and lifecycle markers the old guard burned (363-365, `mail-once.test.ts`) and the slogan on the default share card (366, `share-meta.test.ts`); the review of those decisions added the `REPAIR_BEFORE` cutoff (367, `mail-once.test.ts`). Numbers 368-369 are reserved for that branch. With them `npx vitest run` runs 99 files and 1542 tests on that branch (after merging main on 2026-10-01).

Rule 467 was added, and rules 311 and 313 changed, on 2026-10-02 when chat moved from Opus 5 to Sonnet 5.5 (`CHAT_MODEL`, founder decision, without the golden eval and Gulf-dialect check the pricing spec asked for) and `CHAT_TURN_MAX_USD` fell from $0.35 to $0.14, so `chatOverflowHold()` is 2 credits. All in `tests/chat-allowance.test.ts`.

Rule 510 was added on 2026-10-04 on branch `claude/thakeen-reskin` (re-skin to prototype D, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-04-thakeen-reskin-design.md`). The old Tailwind names (`oasis`, `oasis-deep`, `saffron`, `sadu`, `sadu-deep`, `salt`, `salt-dim`, `mist`, `mist-dim`, `white`) stay in `globals.css` as aliases of D values until the last slice of that branch removes them. Numbers 511-529 are reserved for that branch.

Rules 540-576 were added on 2026-10-05 on branch `claude/security-fixes` (five security fixes, written with their tests, each checked to fail with its fix reverted): the impersonation stop's audit log and rate limit (540-542, `security-auth-fixes.test.ts`), the same-site `next` check shared with Markdown links (543-545) and the reset token moved into the URL fragment (546-547, same file; `routes-auth.test.ts`, `teams-flows.test.ts` and `e2e/auth-flows.spec.ts` now read `#t=`); OAuth and MCP tokens stored as hashes, with atomic GETDEL redemption and the grant as authority (548-555, `security-oauth-fixes.test.ts`); the referrer's bonus moved from signup to the friend's first payment, capped at 10 a month, with its keys deleted with the account (556-559, `security-referral.test.ts`); approval and publish bound to the content the approver saw, the reason capped at 300 characters and preferences.md written under a lock (560-567, `security-approval.test.ts`, with `tests/helpers/version.ts` for the older route tests); chat and MCP brand changes always proposals, and the brand file and crawled site fenced in prompts (568-575, `security-brand-injection.test.ts`; `security-gates.test.ts`, `role-gates.test.ts`, `activity.test.ts`, `activity-sources.test.ts` and `regressions.test.ts` changed on purpose, and the `BEFORE.gen` prompt pins in `cross-review.test.ts` and `handoffs.test.ts` moved); and new media clearing a person's approval (576, `security-approval.test.ts`). Not pinned: a referrer is not clawed back when the friend's payment is voided later, and a payment that `refundPaidTopup` writes through `settle` does not trigger the bonus.
